#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Verify the manuscript's finite claims by exact computations.

The script uses two independent routes on an initial range:

1. enumerate every divisor of F_n and test its rank directly;
2. use B_n = Div(F_n) minus the union of Div(F_{n/p}), compute #B_n by
   inclusion-exclusion, and obtain its minimal elements as minimal threshold
   covers of the prime coordinates of n.

The second route is used on a larger range for the explicit counterexample
search. All arithmetic is exact; no external factor table is used.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import itertools
import math
import platform
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass
from functools import lru_cache
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, Iterable, Optional, Sequence, Tuple

from sympy import __version__ as sympy_version
from sympy import factorint


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BirthLayerResult:
    n: int
    fibonacci: int
    a_count: int
    minimal_generators: Tuple[int, ...]
    birth_layer: Optional[Tuple[int, ...]] = None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RankPureSectorResult:
    n: int
    coordinate_count: int
    exceptional_support_count: int
    minimal_cover_count: int
    admissible_cover_count: int
    connected_admissible_cover_count: int
    canonical_product_count: int
    weighted_product_count: int
    canonical_products: Tuple[int, ...]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RankWindowDeaggregationData:
    n: int
    visible_rank_maximum: int
    prime_window_maximum: int
    multiplicity: int
    exponent_product: int
    exponent_cost: float


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class FibotomicRankEntropyData:
    rank: int
    fibotomic_value: int
    exact_rank_primes: Tuple[int, ...]
    exact_rank_radical: int
    entropy_lower_bound: float
    binet_error: float


@lru_cache(maxsize=None)
def fibonacci(n: int) -> int:
    """Return F_n by fast doubling."""
    if n < 0:
        raise ValueError("n must be nonnegative")

    def doubling(k: int) -> Tuple[int, int]:
        if k == 0:
            return 0, 1
        a, b = doubling(k // 2)
        c = a * (2 * b - a)
        d = a * a + b * b
        return (d, c + d) if k % 2 else (c, d)

    return doubling(n)[0]


@lru_cache(maxsize=None)
def factorint_fibonacci(n: int) -> Tuple[Tuple[int, int], ...]:
    """Return the exact factorization of F_n as an immutable tuple."""
    return tuple(sorted((int(p), int(e)) for p, e in factorint(fibonacci(n)).items()))


def _factorization_text(factors: Sequence[Tuple[int, int]]) -> str:
    if not factors:
        return "1"
    return "*".join(
        str(prime) if exponent == 1 else f"{prime}^{exponent}"
        for prime, exponent in factors
    )


def write_factorization_archive(path: Path, max_n: int) -> None:
    """Write the exact Fibonacci factorizations used by the verification."""
    if max_n < 2:
        raise ValueError("max_n must be at least 2")
    lines = [
        "# python_version\tsympy_version",
        f"# {platform.python_version()}\t{sympy_version}",
        "n\tF_n\tfactorization",
    ]
    for n in range(2, max_n + 1):
        lines.append(
            f"{n}\t{fibonacci(n)}\t{_factorization_text(factorint_fibonacci(n))}"
        )
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    path.write_text("\n".join(lines) + "\n", encoding="ascii")


def load_factorization_archive(
    path: Path, expected_max_n: int
) -> Dict[int, Tuple[Tuple[int, int], ...]]:
    """Load an archive and verify every factorization against F_n."""
    rows: Dict[int, Tuple[Tuple[int, int], ...]] = {}
    for line in path.read_text(encoding="ascii").splitlines():
        if not line or line.startswith("#") or line.startswith("n\t"):
            continue
        n_text, value_text, factors_text = line.split("\t")
        n = int(n_text)
        factors = []
        if factors_text != "1":
            for item in factors_text.split("*"):
                if "^" in item:
                    prime_text, exponent_text = item.split("^", 1)
                    factors.append((int(prime_text), int(exponent_text)))
                else:
                    factors.append((int(item), 1))
        frozen = tuple(factors)
        reconstructed = math.prod(prime**exponent for prime, exponent in frozen)
        if int(value_text) != fibonacci(n) or reconstructed != fibonacci(n):
            raise ValueError(f"invalid factorization archive row for n={n}")
        rows[n] = frozen
    expected = set(range(2, expected_max_n + 1))
    if set(rows) != expected:
        raise ValueError("factorization archive does not contain the expected range")
    return rows


def factor_dict(items: Sequence[Tuple[int, int]]) -> Dict[int, int]:
    return dict(items)


def omega(n: int) -> int:
    """Return the number of distinct prime factors of n."""
    if n <= 1:
        return 0
    return len(factorint(n))


def omega_big(factors: Sequence[Tuple[int, int]]) -> int:
    """Return Omega(N) from the factorization of N."""
    return sum(e for _, e in factors)


def prime_divisors(n: int) -> Tuple[int, ...]:
    return tuple(sorted(int(p) for p in factorint(n)))


def valuation(value: int, prime: int) -> int:
    """Return the prime-adic valuation of a positive integer."""
    exponent = 0
    while value % prime == 0:
        value //= prime
        exponent += 1
    return exponent


def divisors_from_factorization(
    factors: Sequence[Tuple[int, int]],
) -> Tuple[int, ...]:
    divisors = [1]
    for prime, exponent in factors:
        powers = [prime**e for e in range(exponent + 1)]
        divisors = [d * power for d in divisors for power in powers]
    return tuple(sorted(divisors))


def alpha_for_fn_divisor(q: int, n: int) -> int:
    """Compute alpha(q), given q | F_n, by testing the divisors of n."""
    if q == 1:
        return 1
    for d in divisors_from_factorization(tuple(sorted(factorint(n).items()))):
        if fibonacci(d) % q == 0:
            return d
    raise AssertionError(f"{q} does not divide F_{n}")


def minimal_elements(values: Iterable[int]) -> Tuple[int, ...]:
    minima = []
    for value in sorted(set(values)):
        if not any(value % earlier == 0 for earlier in minima):
            minima.append(value)
    return tuple(minima)


@lru_cache(maxsize=None)
def upper_fiber_exhaustive(n: int) -> BirthLayerResult:
    """Enumerate Div(F_n), compute B_n by ranks, and minimize it."""
    if n < 2:
        raise ValueError("birth layers in this paper require n >= 2")
    fn = fibonacci(n)
    divisors = divisors_from_factorization(factorint_fibonacci(n))
    birth_layer = tuple(
        q for q in divisors if q >= 2 and alpha_for_fn_divisor(q, n) == n
    )

    # Independently check the upper-fiber identity on every divisor.
    maximal_proper_fibonacci = tuple(fibonacci(n // p) for p in prime_divisors(n))
    upper_fiber = tuple(
        q
        for q in divisors
        if q >= 2 and all(previous % q != 0 for previous in maximal_proper_fibonacci)
    )
    if birth_layer != upper_fiber:
        raise AssertionError(f"upper-fiber identity failed at n={n}")

    return BirthLayerResult(
        n=n,
        fibonacci=fn,
        a_count=len(birth_layer),
        minimal_generators=minimal_elements(birth_layer),
        birth_layer=birth_layer,
    )


def divisor_count_inside_fn(value: int, fn_factors: Sequence[Tuple[int, int]]) -> int:
    """Return tau(value), knowing value divides the factored F_n."""
    count = 1
    remaining = value
    for prime, _ in fn_factors:
        exponent = valuation(remaining, prime)
        if exponent:
            remaining //= prime**exponent
        count *= exponent + 1
    if remaining != 1:
        raise AssertionError("value contains a prime absent from F_n")
    return count


@lru_cache(maxsize=None)
def upper_fiber_threshold(n: int) -> BirthLayerResult:
    """Compute #B_n and M_n from the upper-fiber threshold constraints."""
    if n < 2:
        raise ValueError("birth layers in this paper require n >= 2")
    fn = fibonacci(n)
    fn_factors = factorint_fibonacci(n)
    coordinates = prime_divisors(n)
    if not fn_factors:
        return BirthLayerResult(n, fn, 0, tuple(), None)

    # A(n) by inclusion-exclusion over the covering ideals D_{n/p}.
    a_count = 0
    for size in range(len(coordinates) + 1):
        for chosen in itertools.combinations(coordinates, size):
            divisor = math.prod(chosen)
            index = n // divisor
            term = divisor_count_inside_fn(fibonacci(index), fn_factors)
            a_count += -term if size % 2 else term

    # For each coordinate ell | n, q lies outside D_{n/ell} exactly when
    # one prime exponent of q exceeds its exponent in F_{n/ell}.
    choices = []
    for ell in coordinates:
        previous = fibonacci(n // ell)
        coordinate_choices = []
        for prime, exponent in fn_factors:
            threshold = valuation(previous, prime) + 1
            if threshold <= exponent:
                coordinate_choices.append((prime, threshold))
        if not coordinate_choices:
            raise AssertionError(f"coordinate {ell} has no witness at n={n}")
        choices.append(tuple(coordinate_choices))

    candidates = set()
    for selection in itertools.product(*choices):
        exponents: Dict[int, int] = {}
        for prime, threshold in selection:
            exponents[prime] = max(exponents.get(prime, 0), threshold)
        candidate = math.prod(prime**exponent for prime, exponent in exponents.items())
        candidates.add(candidate)

    minima = minimal_elements(candidates)
    return BirthLayerResult(n, fn, a_count, minima, None)


def _support_pairs(m: int, n: int):
    n_factorization = factor_dict(tuple(sorted((int(p), int(e)) for p, e in factorint(n).items())))
    coordinates = tuple(n_factorization)
    pairs = []
    for prime, exponent in sorted((int(p), int(e)) for p, e in factorint(m).items()):
        theta = prime**exponent
        lowered = prime ** (exponent - 1)
        rank = alpha_for_fn_divisor(theta, n)
        lower_rank = alpha_for_fn_divisor(lowered, n)
        full = frozenset(
            i
            for i, ell in enumerate(coordinates)
            if valuation(rank, ell) == n_factorization[ell]
        )
        essential = frozenset(
            i
            for i in full
            if valuation(lower_rank, coordinates[i]) < n_factorization[coordinates[i]]
        )
        pairs.append((essential, full))
    return tuple(pairs)


def _normalized_pairs(pairs) -> Tuple[Tuple[Tuple[int, ...], Tuple[int, ...]], ...]:
    return tuple(
        sorted((tuple(sorted(essential)), tuple(sorted(full))) for essential, full in pairs)
    )


def classify_support_three(m: int, n: int) -> str:
    """Classify a support-three minimal generator into Gamma_1,...,Gamma_9."""
    if omega(n) != 3:
        raise ValueError("support-three classification requires omega(n) = 3")
    actual = _normalized_pairs(_support_pairs(m, n))
    templates = {
        "Gamma_1": [({0, 1, 2}, {0, 1, 2})],
        "Gamma_2": [({0, 1}, {0, 1, 2})],
        "Gamma_3": [({0}, {0, 1, 2})],
        "Gamma_4": [({0, 1}, {0, 1}), ({1, 2}, {1, 2})],
        "Gamma_5": [({0}, {0, 1}), ({1, 2}, {1, 2})],
        "Gamma_6": [({0}, {0, 1}), ({2}, {1, 2})],
        "Gamma_7": [({0}, {0}), ({1, 2}, {1, 2})],
        "Gamma_8": [({0}, {0}), ({1}, {1, 2})],
        "Gamma_9": [({0}, {0}), ({1}, {1}), ({2}, {2})],
    }
    matches = []
    for label, template in templates.items():
        for permutation in itertools.permutations(range(3)):
            permuted = [
                (
                    frozenset(permutation[i] for i in essential),
                    frozenset(permutation[i] for i in full),
                )

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Regression tests for the Deepening Delta verification battery."""

import math
import tempfile
import unittest
from pathlib import Path

from sympy import factorint

from verify_finite_claims import (
    atomic_family_multiplicity,
    bell_number,
    bicolored_graph_count,

    exact_rank_prime_count,

    fibotomic_error_bound,

class FiniteClaimTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_fibotomic_rank_entropy_and_rank_congruences_through_120(self):
        error_bound = fibotomic_error_bound()
        for rank in range(3, 121):
            with self.subTest(rank=rank):
                data = fibotomic_rank_entropy_data(rank)
                self.assertEqual(
                    len(data.exact_rank_primes), exact_rank_prime_count(rank)
                )
                self.assertEqual(
                    data.exact_rank_radical,
                    math.prod(data.exact_rank_primes),
                )
                self.assertEqual(
                    data.fibotomic_value % data.exact_rank_radical, 0
                )
                self.assertLessEqual(
                    data.entropy_lower_bound,
                    math.log(data.fibotomic_value) + 1e-12,
                )
                self.assertLessEqual(abs(data.binet_error), error_bound)
                for index, prime in enumerate(data.exact_rank_primes, start=1):
                    self.assertGreaterEqual(
                        prime, rank * math.ceil(index / 2) - 1
                    )
                    if prime not in (2, 5):
                        self.assertTrue(
                            (prime - 1) % rank == 0
                            or (prime + 1) % rank == 0
                        )

    def test_jarden_exact_rank_consequence_in_available_range(self):
        for prime in (7, 11, 13, 17, 19):
            with self.subTest(prime=prime):
                self.assertGreaterEqual(exact_rank_prime_count(10 * prime), 2)

    def test_rank_window_deaggregation_bounds_through_120(self):
        for n in range(3, 121):
            with self.subTest(n=n):
                data = rank_window_deaggregation_data(n)
                self.assertLessEqual(data.prime_window_maximum, data.multiplicity)
                self.assertLessEqual(
                    data.multiplicity, data.prime_window_maximum + 1
                )
                self.assertLessEqual(data.visible_rank_maximum, data.multiplicity)
                self.assertLessEqual(
                    data.multiplicity,
                    1 + data.visible_rank_maximum * data.exponent_product,
                )
                self.assertGreaterEqual(
                    math.log(data.multiplicity)
                    - math.log(data.visible_rank_maximum),
                    0.0,
                )
                self.assertLessEqual(
                    math.log(data.multiplicity)
                    - math.log(data.visible_rank_maximum),
                    math.log(2) + data.exponent_cost + 1e-12,
                )

    def test_squarefree_exact_rank_partition_and_blms_pigeonhole(self):
        checks = 0
        for n in range(3, 121):
            factors = factorint(n)
            if any(exponent != 1 for exponent in factors.values()):
                continue
            data = rank_window_deaggregation_data(n)
            exact_rank_total = sum(
                exact_rank_prime_count(d)
                for d in range(1, n + 1)
                if n % d == 0
            )
            self.assertEqual(exact_rank_total, len(factorint_fibonacci(n)))
            self.assertGreaterEqual(
                data.multiplicity,
                exact_rank_total / (2 ** omega(n) - 1),
            )
            checks += 1
        self.assertEqual(checks, 73)

    def test_refined_private_cover_bound_and_boundary_convention(self):
        self.assertEqual(atomic_family_multiplicity(2), 1)
        self.assertEqual(refined_private_cover_upper_bound(1, 1), 2)
        self.assertEqual(refined_private_cover_upper_bound(2, 1), 10)
        self.assertEqual(refined_private_cover_upper_bound(3, 1), 50)
        for support_size in range(1, 20):
            for multiplicity in (1, 2, 5):
                self.assertLessEqual(
                    refined_private_cover_upper_bound(support_size, multiplicity),
                    multiplicity**support_size
                    * refined_private_cover_upper_bound(support_size, 1),
                )

    def test_minimal_cover_formula_and_connected_counts(self):
        self.assertEqual(
            tuple(minimal_cover_count(k) for k in range(1, 7)),
            (1, 2, 8, 49, 462, 6424),
        )
        self.assertEqual(minimal_cover_counts_by_size(5), (1, 90, 305, 65, 1))
        self.assertEqual(
            tuple(connected_minimal_cover_count(k) for k in range(1, 6)),
            (1, 1, 4, 23, 241),
        )
        self.assertEqual(bicolored_graph_count(4), 162)

    def test_theta_asymptotic_and_local_limit_numerically(self):
        self.assertAlmostEqual(theta_constant(0), 2.128936827211877, places=14)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(theta_constant(1), 2.128931250513028, places=14)
        for parity in (0, 1):
            ratios = [
                theta_normalized_cover_ratio(k)
                for k in range(20 + parity, 81, 2)
            ]
            self.assertLess(abs(ratios[-1] - 1.0), abs(ratios[0] - 1.0))
            self.assertLess(abs(ratios[-1] - 1.0), 0.02)
        for d in (-2, -1, 0, 1, 2):
            even_actual, even_limit = local_limit_probability(80, d)
            odd_actual, odd_limit = local_limit_probability(81, d)
            self.assertLess(abs(even_actual - even_limit), 0.01)
            self.assertLess(abs(odd_actual - odd_limit), 0.01)

    def test_rank_pure_sector_handles_exceptional_and_odd_layers(self):
        even = rank_pure_sector(30)
        self.assertEqual(even.coordinate_count, 3)
        self.assertEqual(even.exceptional_support_count, 2)
        self.assertEqual(even.minimal_cover_count, 8)
        self.assertEqual(even.admissible_cover_count, 3)
        self.assertEqual(even.canonical_product_count, 3)

        odd = rank_pure_sector(105)
        self.assertEqual(odd.coordinate_count, 3)
        self.assertEqual(odd.exceptional_support_count, 0)
        self.assertEqual(odd.minimal_cover_count, 8)
        self.assertEqual(odd.admissible_cover_count, 8)
        self.assertEqual(odd.canonical_product_count, 8)

    def test_rank_pure_products_are_independent_minimal_generators(self):
        for n in (30, 42, 105, 210):
            with self.subTest(n=n):
                sector = rank_pure_sector(n)
                actual = set(upper_fiber_threshold(n).minimal_generators)
                self.assertEqual(
                    sector.canonical_product_count,
                    len(sector.canonical_products),
                )
                self.assertTrue(set(sector.canonical_products).issubset(actual))
                self.assertGreaterEqual(
                    sector.weighted_product_count,
                    sector.canonical_product_count,
                )

    def test_corrected_n30_data_and_types(self):
        exhaustive = upper_fiber_exhaustive(30)
        threshold = upper_fiber_threshold(30)

        expected = (20, 22, 31, 244, 671)
        self.assertEqual(exhaustive.a_count, 52)
        self.assertEqual(exhaustive.minimal_generators, expected)
        self.assertEqual(threshold.a_count, 52)
        self.assertEqual(threshold.minimal_generators, expected)

        realized = {
            classify_support_three(m, 30) for m in expected
        }
        self.assertEqual(
            realized,
            {"Gamma_1", "Gamma_4", "Gamma_5", "Gamma_7", "Gamma_8"},
        )
        self.assertTrue(
            {"Gamma_3", "Gamma_6", "Gamma_9"}.isdisjoint(realized)
        )

    def test_independent_methods_agree_through_30(self):
        for n in range(2, 31):
            with self.subTest(n=n):
                exhaustive = upper_fiber_exhaustive(n)
                threshold = upper_fiber_threshold(n)
                self.assertEqual(exhaustive.a_count, threshold.a_count)
                self.assertEqual(
                    exhaustive.minimal_generators,
                    threshold.minimal_generators,
                )

    def test_finite_growth_bounds_through_50(self):
        for n in range(3, 51):
            with self.subTest(n=n):
                result = upper_fiber_threshold(n)
                k = omega(n)
                big_omega = omega_big(factorint_fibonacci(n))
                subset_bound = sum(
                    math.comb(big_omega, r)
                    for r in range(0, min(k, big_omega) + 1)
                )
                self.assertLessEqual(len(result.minimal_generators), subset_bound)
                self.assertLessEqual(len(result.minimal_generators), n**k)
                if n % 2 == 1:
                    self.assertGreaterEqual(
                        len(result.minimal_generators), bell_number(k)
                    )

    def test_private_cover_bounds_through_120(self):
        for n in range(3, 121):
            with self.subTest(n=n):
                result = upper_fiber_threshold(n)
                k = omega(n)
                count = len(result.minimal_generators)
                multiplicity = atomic_family_multiplicity(n)
                self.assertGreaterEqual(multiplicity, 1)
                if k >= 3:
                    self.assertGreaterEqual(count, private_cover_lower_bound(k))
                self.assertLessEqual(
                    count, private_cover_upper_bound(k, multiplicity)
                )

    def test_private_cover_bounds_have_the_claimed_finite_values(self):
        self.assertEqual(private_cover_lower_bound(3), 1)
        self.assertEqual(private_cover_lower_bound(4), 9)
        self.assertEqual(private_cover_lower_bound(5), 27)
        self.assertEqual(private_cover_lower_bound(6), 343)
        self.assertEqual(private_cover_upper_bound(1, 1), 2)
        self.assertEqual(private_cover_upper_bound(2, 1), 12)

    def test_factorization_archive_round_trip_through_30(self):
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
            path = Path(tmpdir) / "fibonacci_factorizations_2_30.tsv"
            write_factorization_archive(path, 30)
            archive = load_factorization_archive(path, 30)
            self.assertEqual(archive[2], tuple())
            self.assertEqual(archive[30], factorint_fibonacci(30))
            text = path.read_text(encoding="ascii")
            self.assertIn("python_version\tsympy_version", text)
            self.assertIn("30\t832040\t2^3*5*11*31*61", text)

    def test_report_documents_set_equalities_and_versions(self):
        report = run_battery(30, 30)
        self.assertIn("B_n direct = B_n upper fiber: 29/29 set equalities", report)
        self.assertIn("M_n direct = M_n witness: 29/29 set equalities", report)
        self.assertIn("Rank-pure layers checked: 28", report)
        self.assertIn("Odd layers realizing all minimal covers: 14/14", report)
        self.assertIn("Rank-pure canonical products in M_n: 28/28 layer checks", report)
        self.assertIn("Theta-normalized C_k ratios at k=20,40,80:", report)
        self.assertIn("Central local-limit errors at k=40,80 (d=0):", report)
        self.assertIn("Rank-window deaggregation inequalities: 28/28", report)
        self.assertIn("Squarefree BLMS pigeonhole inequalities: 17/17", report)
        self.assertIn("Refined private-cover upper bounds: 28/28", report)
        self.assertIn("Fibotomic rank-entropy inequalities: 28/28", report)
        self.assertIn("Fibotomic exact-rank radical divisibilities: 28/28", report)
        self.assertIn("Jarden a(10p) >= 2 checks: 0/0", report)
        self.assertIn("Python version:", report)
        self.assertIn("SymPy version:", report)
        self.assertNotIn("Deepening Delta", report)
        self.assertNotIn("counterexample battery", report.lower())

\section{Rank-pure secondary asymptotics}
\label{sec:supp-rank-pure-asymptotics}

\begin{lemma}[Connected minimal-cover concentration]
\label{lem:connected-minimal-cover-concentration}
If $C_k^{\rm conn}$ denotes the number of connected minimal covers, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:connected-minimal-cover-asymptotic}
 C_k-C_k^{\rm conn}=O(kC_{k-1})=o(C_k).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Connected components of a minimal cover are themselves minimal covers, so the
labelled exponential formula gives
\[
 C_k=\sum_{j=1}^k\binom{k-1}{j-1}C_j^{\rm conn}C_{k-j}.
\]
The terms $j=1$ and $j=k-1$ contribute $O(kC_{k-1})$. Choosing the least
private element in each cover member gives $C_j\le b_j\le2^j2^{j^2/4}$.
For the remaining terms, use this bound and the central lower bound for
$C_{k-1}$ furnished by the $s=\lfloor(k-1)/2\rfloor$ term of
\eqref{article-eq:hearne-wagner}. After division by $kC_{k-1}$, the term indexed by
$j$ is
\[
 \ll \binom{k}{j}
 2^{-(j-1)(k-j-1)/2}\qquad(2\le j\le k-2).
\]
The sum of these bounds is bounded (and in fact tends to zero), by symmetry
and separation into $2\le j\le k/2$ and its reflection. This proves the first
estimate in \eqref{eq:connected-minimal-cover-asymptotic}; the second equality
follows from \eqref{article-eq:minimal-cover-theta}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Theta-refined connected support entropy]
\label{cor:theta-refined-connected-entropy}
Uniformly along every sequence of integers for which $k=\omega(n)\to\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:theta-connected-lower-bound}
 \#\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}\ge
 (1-o(1))\,
 \vartheta_\varepsilon\binom{k}{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}2^{k^2/4},
 \qquad \varepsilon\equiv k\pmod2.
\end{equation}
Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:theta-connected-log-lower-bound}
 \log\#\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}\ge
 \frac{\log2}{4}k^2+k\log2-\frac12\log k
 +\log\vartheta_\varepsilon+\frac12\log\frac2\pi+o(1).
\end{equation}
The same lower bounds hold with $\mathcal M_n$ in place of
$\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Fix an exceptional support $S_0\in\mathcal E_n$. If a minimal cover contains
$S_0$, choose a canonical private vertex $x\in S_0$ of that member. Every
other member avoids $x$ and is recovered from its canonical private vertex and
an arbitrary subset of the remaining nonprivate vertices. The number of such
encodings is at most $b_{k-1}$ for each choice of $x$. Therefore the number of
minimal covers meeting $\mathcal E_n$ is at most
\[
 \sum_{S_0\in\mathcal E_n}|S_0|b_{k-1}\le4b_{k-1}=o(C_k).
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:connected-minimal-cover-concentration} also shows that only
$o(C_k)$ minimal covers are disconnected. Thus $(1-o(1))C_k$ covers are simultaneously
connected and disjoint from $\mathcal E_n$. Theorem
\ref{article-thm:rank-pure-universality} embeds them in
$\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}$, proving \eqref{eq:theta-connected-lower-bound}.
Taking logarithms and using
\[
 \log\binom{k}{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}
 =k\log2-\frac12\log k+\frac12\log\frac2\pi+o(1)
\]
gives \eqref{eq:theta-connected-log-lower-bound}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Discrete local limit in a fixed-prime rank-pure sector]
\label{cor:rank-pure-local-limit}
Let $(n_j)$ be any sequence with $k_j=\omega(n_j)\to\infty$. For every
$j$ and every nonexceptional nonempty support
$S\subseteq\mathcal P(n_j)$, fix one prime
$\pi_{j,S}\in\Pi_\alpha((n_j)_S)$. Let $\mathcal Q_{n_j}^{\rm rp}$ consist of the
products $\prod_{S\in\mathcal C}\pi_{j,S}$ over all
$\mathcal C\in\operatorname{MinCov}(\mathcal P(n_j))$ disjoint from
$\mathcal E_{n_j}$, and choose $m$ uniformly from $\mathcal Q_{n_j}^{\rm rp}$. Put
$W_j=\omega(m)$. Along the subsequence $k_j=2q_j$, for every fixed
$d\in\mathbb Z$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:even-rank-pure-local-limit}
 \Pr(W_j=q_j+d)\longrightarrow\frac{2^{-d^2}}{\vartheta_0}.
\end{equation}
Along the subsequence $k_j=2q_j+1$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:odd-rank-pure-local-limit}
 \Pr(W_j=q_j+d)\longrightarrow
 \frac{2^{-(d-1/2)^2}}{\vartheta_1}.
\end{equation}
In particular, $W_j=k_j/2+O_{\mathbb P}(1)$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Unique factorization and distinct support ranks show that $W_j$ is the number
of members of the corresponding cover. By the proof of
Corollary~\ref{cor:theta-refined-connected-entropy}, deleting covers meeting
$\mathcal E_{n_j}$ changes the uniform distribution in total variation by
$o(1)$. For $s=\lfloor k/2\rfloor+d$ with fixed $d$, the $j=s$ term of
\eqref{article-eq:hearne-wagner} and the canonical-private-element encoding give
\[
 \binom{k}{s}(2^s-s-1)^{k-s}
 \le C_{k,s}\le \binom{k}{s}2^{s(k-s)},
 \qquad
 C_{k,s}\sim\binom{k}{s}2^{s(k-s)}.
\]
Dividing this by \eqref{article-eq:minimal-cover-theta} gives
\eqref{eq:even-rank-pure-local-limit} and
\eqref{eq:odd-rank-pure-local-limit}. The quadratic limiting tails are
summable, which is equivalent to tightness on the integer scale.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:connected-lower-not-dominance}
Corollary~\ref{cor:theta-refined-connected-entropy} proves that connected
minimal generators alone realize the theta-refined lower bound supplied by the
unweighted minimal-cover count in the fixed-choice rank-pure sector, including
its linear and logarithmic corrections. It does not compare the total connected
and disconnected parts of $\mathcal M_n$, nor does it assert that this sector
exhausts the consequences of fibotomic information. Such a comparison would
require upper control of the arithmetic multiplicities in $R(n)$ and is not
presently available.
\end{remark}



 succeeded in 152ms:
\section{Secondary arithmetic bounds}
\label{sec:supp-arithmetic-bounds}

\begin{lemma}[Known baseline for the arithmetic interface]
\label{lem:R-baseline}
For every $n\ge3$,
\[
 R(n)\le 1+\omega(F_n)=O(n),
 \qquad
 \log R(n)\le\log n+O(1).
\]
Moreover, Stroi\'nski's cumulative estimate gives
\[
 \limsup_{x\to\infty}\frac{\log x}{x^2}
 \#\{p:\alpha(p)\le x\}
 \le \frac{3\log\varphi}{2\pi^2}.
\]
Neither estimate implies the conjectures below.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Every prime atom in a family $\mathcal A_{I,J}(n)$ divides $F_n$, and a fixed
ladder family contains at most one atom. Thus a family contains at most all
distinct prime divisors of $F_n$ together with one ladder atom, proving
$R(n)\le1+\omega(F_n)$. Since
$2^{\omega(F_n)}\le\operatorname{rad}(F_n)\le F_n$ and
$\log F_n=n\log\varphi+O(1)$, this gives $R(n)=O(n)$ and the logarithmic
bound. The cumulative estimate is Stroi\'nski's Theorem~7
\cite{Stroinski2016AlphaContraction}.

Quantitatively, the normal-order conjecture below asks for
$\log R(n)=o(\log\log n)$ for almost all $n$, whereas the elementary estimate
gives only $O(\log n)$. The cumulative estimate controls all primes of rank at
most $x$ together and likewise gives at best a logarithmic bound of order
$\log n$ for a single window. For the mean conjecture, the elementary bound
and the mean order of $\omega$ give only
$O(x\log x\log\log x)$, not $o(x(\log\log x)^2)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Cumulative rank estimates do not imply \textup{(H2)}]
\label{rem:cumulative-rank-estimates-insufficient}
This nonimplication can be made quantitative without asserting that the model
below is realized by Fibonacci ranks. Fix $0<c<\log\varphi/2$ and put
\[
 a_0(d):=\max\left\{1,
 \left\lfloor\frac{c\phi_{\rm E}(d)}{\log(2d)}\right\rfloor\right\}.
\]
Elementary integral comparison gives
\[
 \sum_{d\le y}a_0(d)\ll\frac{y^2}{\log y},\qquad
 a_0(d)\ll1+\frac{\phi_{\rm E}(d)}{\log(2d)},\qquad
 \sum_{d\le y}a_0(d)\log d\ll y^2.
\]
Thus this formal multiplicity sequence has the cumulative, pointwise, and
logarithmic-mass scales supplied by the presently known estimates, including
Theorem~\ref{article-thm:fibotomic-rank-entropy}. It is not asserted to arise from
Fibonacci exact-rank primes, and therefore it neither proves sharpness of the
fibotomic inequality nor supplies a realizable extremal model for it. On the
other hand, the standard
lower bound for Euler's function gives
\[
 \log a_0(n)=\log n-\log\log n+O(\log\log\log(3n))
\]
for large $n$.
Partial summation in \eqref{article-eq:mean-omega-input} therefore gives
\[
 \sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)\log a_0(n)
 \sim x\log x\log\log x,
\]
which is larger than the scale in \textup{(H2)}. Hence cumulative exact-rank
counts alone cannot establish the required visible-divisor maximum estimate.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Unconditional lower growth]
\label{cor:unconditional-lower-growth}
For almost all $n$,
\[
 \log\#\mathcal M_n\ge
 \left(\frac{\log2}{4}+o(1)\right)(\log\log n)^2.
\]
If $N_y:=\prod_{p\le y}p$, then
\[
 \log\#\mathcal M_{N_y}\ge
 \left(\frac{\log2}{4}+o(1)\right)
 \frac{(\log N_y)^2}{(\log\log N_y)^2}.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The Hardy--Ramanujan normal-order theorem states that
$\omega(n)=(1+o(1))\log\log n$ for almost all $n$
\cite{HardyRamanujan1917NormalPrimeFactors,%
Tenenbaum2015AnalyticProbabilistic}. Substitution in the lower half of
\eqref{article-eq:private-cover-entropy-order} proves the first assertion. The prime
number theorem gives
$\omega(N_y)=\pi(y)\sim y/\log y\sim\log N_y/\log\log N_y$; substituting this
in the same lower bound proves the second assertion.
\end{proof}

\section{Visible single-rank bounds}
\label{sec:supp-visible-rank-bounds}

\begin{corollary}[Bounds for the visible single-rank contribution]
\label{cor:visible-rank-sum-bounds}
Put
\[
 S(x):=\sum_{3\leq n\leq x}\omega(n)\log A^*(n).
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:visible-rank-sum-upper}
 S(x)\leq
 \sum_{3\leq n\leq x}\omega(n)\log\phi_{\rm E}(n)
 -x(\log\log x)^2+O(x\log\log x),
\end{equation}
whereas
\begin{equation}\label{eq:visible-rank-sum-lower}
 S(x)\geq
 \left(\frac{2\log2}{25}+o(1)\right)x\log\log x.
\end{equation}
Neither estimate settles \eqref{article-eq:visible-single-rank-interface}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Taking logarithms in \eqref{article-eq:visible-exact-rank-bound}, uniformly for
large $n$, gives
\[
 \log A^*(n)\leq
 \log\phi_{\rm E}(n)-\log\log n
 +\log\frac{\log\varphi}{2}+o(1).
\]
In addition to \eqref{article-eq:mean-omega-input}, partial summation gives
\[
 \sum_{3\leq n\leq x}\omega(n)\log\log n
 =x(\log\log x)^2+O(x\log\log x).
\]
For example, on $\sqrt x<n\leq x$ one may replace $\log\log n$ by
$\log\log x$ with $O(1)$ error, while the complementary range contributes
$o(x)$. Summation proves \eqref{eq:visible-rank-sum-upper}.

For the lower bound, Jarden proved that $L_{5p}$ has at least two distinct
primitive prime divisors for every prime $p>5$
\cite{Jarden1968LucasTwoPrimitive}; this published theorem is used as an
input and is not asserted as a result of the present paper. If $q$ is one of
them, then
$q\mid L_{5p}$ and
\[
 F_{10p}=F_{5p}L_{5p},\qquad \gcd(F_{5p},L_{5p})\mid2.
\]
Here $q$ is odd, so the gcd identity gives $q\nmid F_{5p}$. Thus
$\alpha(q)=2r$ for some $r\mid5p$. If $r<5p$, then
$F_{2r}=F_rL_r$ and $q\nmid F_r$, so $q\mid L_r$, contrary to the
primitivity of $q$ for $L_{5p}$.
Consequently $\alpha(q)=10p$, and therefore
\begin{equation}\label{eq:jarden-exact-rank-consequence}
 a(10p)\geq2\qquad(p>5\ \hbox{prime}).
\end{equation}

Let $n=10m$, where $5\nmid m$ and $m$ has a prime factor $p>5$. Then
$d=10p$ divides $n$ and
$\nu_5(d)=\nu_5(n)=1$, so $T_n(d)\ne\varnothing$. By
\eqref{eq:jarden-exact-rank-consequence}, $A^*(n)\geq2$. The excluded
integers $m$ having no prime factor greater than $5$ are of the form
$2^a3^b$ and contribute only $O((\log x)^3)$ after weighting by $\omega$.
Writing $y=x/10$ and interchanging two finite sums gives
\[
 \sum_{\substack{m\leq y\\5\nmid m}}\omega(m)
 =\sum_{\substack{q\leq y\\q\ \mathrm{prime},\ q\ne5}}
 \left(\left\lfloor\frac yq\right\rfloor
 -\left\lfloor\frac y{5q}\right\rfloor\right)
 =\frac45y\log\log y+O(y).
\]
Since $\omega(10m)=\omega(m)+O(1)$, the total $\omega$-weight of the
constructed integers is
$(2/25)x\log\log x+O(x)$. Multiplication by $\log2$ proves
\eqref{eq:visible-rank-sum-lower}.
\end{proof}


For a finite set $V$, let $\operatorname{MinCov}(V)$ denote the set of
minimal, or irredundant, covers of $V$ by nonempty subsets:
\[
 \operatorname{MinCov}(V):=
 \left\{\mathcal C\subseteq 2^V\setminus\{\varnothing\}:
 \bigcup_{S\in\mathcal C}S=V,\quad
 S\nsubseteq\bigcup_{T\in\mathcal C\setminus\{S\}}T
 \ \text{for every }S\in\mathcal C\right\}.
\]



 succeeded in 152ms:
\section{Numerical evidence}\label{sec:numerical}
%% ====================================================================

Table~\ref{tab:birth-layer-data-generated} displays the birth layer cardinality $A(n)$
and the number of minimal generators $\#\mathcal M_n$ for $2\le n\le 30$,
computed by exhaustive enumeration. The table is illustrative only: the
unconditional inequalities in Theorem~\ref{article-thm:minimal-generator-growth} do
not rely on this finite sample. Several features visible in the
data agree with the structural results of the preceding sections.
First, $\#\mathcal M_n=1$ whenever $\omega(n)=1$ and
$n\notin\{19,25,27\}$; the exceptions arise from composite Fibonacci numbers
with multiple atomic families at the same prime-power index.
Second, the jump at $n=12$ ($A(12)=10$, $\#\mathcal M_{12}=3$) and at $n=24$
($A(24)=55$, $\#\mathcal M_{24}=4$) reflects the interaction of the
exceptional prime $2$ with the layers having no exact-rank prime at indices
$6$ and $12$.
Third, the first nonempty three-coordinate layer occurs at $n=30$ and has
$\#\mathcal M_{30}=5$; the displayed witness covers realize exactly the
types $\Gamma_1$, $\Gamma_4$, $\Gamma_5$, $\Gamma_7$, and $\Gamma_8$.
The remaining admissible shapes $\Gamma_3$, $\Gamma_6$, and $\Gamma_9$ are
not realized at $n=30$. This exact five-type assertion, including the three
nonrealization proofs, is Proposition~\ref{prop:n30-five-type-classification};
it is not inferred from the table.

The table was produced by the finite divisor/rank computation in the next
proposition, applied exhaustively for $2\le n\le30$.  The manuscript source
includes the short script that performs this enumeration and the generated
table file input below; these files are supplementary to the mathematical
criterion rather than part of the theorem stream. For the classical
factorization literature, see the tables of Brillhart--Montgomery--Silverman
\cite{BrillhartMontgomerySilverman1988Tables}.

\begin{proposition}[Finite divisor/rank verification with supplied factorization]
\label{prop:effective-birth-layer-computation}
Let $n\ge2$ and suppose the integer factorization of $F_n$ is known. Then
$B_n$, $A(n)$, and $\mathcal M_n$ are recovered exactly by the following finite
divisor/rank test.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item Enumerate the divisor set $D_n=\operatorname{Div}(F_n)$ from the
 factorization of $F_n$.
\item Enumerate the divisor set $\operatorname{Div}(n)$. For each
 $q\in D_n$, compute
 \[
 \alpha_n(q):=\min\{d\mid n:q\mid F_d\}.
 \]
\item Set $B_n=\{q\in D_n:q\ge2,\ \alpha_n(q)=n\}$ and
 $A(n)=\#B_n$.
\item Sort $B_n$ by divisibility-compatible order, for instance by size, and
 retain exactly those $m\in B_n$ for which no previously retained element
 divides $m$; the retained set is $\mathcal M_n$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since every $q\in D_n$ divides $F_n$, Proposition~\ref{article-prop:rank-basics}
shows that $q\mid F_d$ for some $d$ if and only if $\alpha(q)\mid d$. In
particular $q\mid F_n$ implies $\alpha(q)\mid n$, so the least positive index
at which $q$ appears is exactly
\[
\alpha(q)=\min\{d\mid n:q\mid F_d\}=\alpha_n(q).
\]
Hence the test in step (c) gives precisely the definition
$B_n=\{q\in D_n:\alpha(q)=n\}$, with the harmless exclusion of $q=1$ already
consistent with the convention $\alpha(1)=1$ and $n\ge2$. Counting this set
gives $A(n)$.

It remains to justify step (d). The set $B_n$ is finite. If its elements are
processed in increasing size, then every proper divisor of the current element
has already been processed. We claim inductively that every discarded element
has a retained minimal divisor. The claim is vacuous before the first element.
If the current element is discarded, it has a previously retained divisor by
the algorithm, so the claim holds for it; if it is retained, it is its own
retained divisor. Thus every earlier divisor, retained or discarded, has a
retained divisor below it. Consequently the current element is retained if and
only if no smaller element of $B_n$ divides it, which is exactly minimality in
the divisibility poset. The retained set is therefore $\mathcal M_n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Reproducibility scope]
The preceding proposition is used only to document the finite verification
interface for the table, not as a complexity theorem.  After the factorization
of $F_n$ has been supplied, the displayed loops involve only the finite divisor
sets of $F_n$ and $n$, ordinary divisibility tests $q\mid F_d$, and divisor
comparisons inside $B_n$.  The manuscript makes no bit-complexity assertion and
does not include the cost of factoring $F_n$ in the numerical evidence.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Table generation.}
The reproducibility files
\[
\text{\texttt{artifacts/compute\_birth\_layer\_table.py}},\qquad
\text{\texttt{artifacts/tab\_birth\_layer\_data.tex}}
\]
are included with the manuscript source. Running the command in
\texttt{artifacts/REPRODUCE.md}
\[
\texttt{python artifacts/compute\_birth\_layer\_table.py}
\]
regenerates the table input from the divisor/rank method of
Proposition~\ref{prop:effective-birth-layer-computation}.  The printed table is
therefore an exhaustive enumeration of $A(n)$ and $\#\mathcal M_n$ for
$2\le n\le30$ using only the finite tests proved above; the displayed
factorizations of the small integers $F_n$ are produced by the script's exact
integer trial-division routine. Its complete console output is supplied as
\path{artifacts/birth_layer_table_output.txt}.
\input{generated/tab_birth_layer_data.tex}

\paragraph{Finite verification.}
The supplementary script
\texttt{artifacts/verify\_finite\_claims.py} independently compares the actual
sets obtained by direct divisor/rank enumeration with the upper-fiber and
witness constructions through $n=60$. It
then uses the latter formula through $n=210$: inclusion--exclusion computes
\[
 A(n)=\sum_{S\subseteq\mathcal P(n)}(-1)^{|S|}
 \tau\!\left(F_{n/\prod_{p\in S}p}\right),
\]
and the minimal threshold covers of the conditions
$q\nmid F_{n/p}$ compute $\mathcal M_n$.  The script checks
\eqref{article-eq:finite-minimal-generator-bound}, the simplified bound
$\#\mathcal M_n\le n^{\omega(n)}$, the odd Bell lower bound, and both finite
private-cover bounds in \eqref{article-eq:private-cover-finite-bounds}. It also
computes $R(n)$, checks the pointwise deaggregation bounds
\eqref{article-eq:prime-window-versus-R} and
\eqref{article-eq:pointwise-window-deaggregation}, verifies the exact-rank partition
and \eqref{article-eq:squarefree-blms-pigeonhole} on every squarefree layer, checks
the refined upper bound \eqref{eq:refined-private-coordinate-upper-bound},
checks the fibotomic identities \eqref{article-eq:fibotomic-binet-error} and
\eqref{article-eq:exact-rank-radical-divides-fibotomic}, the entropy inequality
\eqref{article-eq:fibotomic-rank-entropy}, the exact-rank congruence candidate bound,
and the available cases of \eqref{eq:jarden-exact-rank-consequence}, and
records the running ratio against the support-entropy scale
$(\log2)\omega(n)^2/4$. For the rank-pure results, it directly enumerates all
minimal covers for $k\le4$, checks the Hearne--Wagner and connected-component
counts, verifies the M\"obius exact-rank-prime formula on every support, and
tests every canonical rank-pure product against the independently computed
$\mathcal M_n$. This covers all $208$ layers $3\le n\le210$, including exact
realization of every minimal cover on all $104$ odd layers. It reports $59/59$
set equalities for both original comparisons and no finite-bound or rank-pure
failures. The exact output is
\path{artifacts/finite_verification.txt}. The exact factorizations used for
$2\le n\le210$ are recorded in
\path{artifacts/fibonacci_factorizations_2_210.tsv}; the verifier checks
every row by multiplication. The Python and SymPy versions, commands, file
inventory, and verification scope are in \path{artifacts/REPRODUCE.md}, and
\path{artifacts/SHA256SUMS} authenticates the supplied files. These finite
checks do not provide distributional control of $R(n)$.
In particular, they do not prove the asymptotic hypotheses \textup{(H2)} or
\textup{(BW)}.

\section{Finite verification for the three-coordinate table}
\label{app:three-coordinate-checklist}

\begin{proposition}[Reader's checklist for the three-coordinate table]
\label{prop:three-coordinate-table-checklist}
Assume $\mathcal P(n)=\{\ell_1,
\ell_2,\ell_3\}$.  The following finite procedure returns exactly
$\mathcal M_n$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item For every nonempty $J\subseteq[3]$, compute the exact-rank window
$\mathcal R_J^\circ(n)$ and the rank classes
$\Pi_\alpha(d)$ for $d\in\mathcal R_J^\circ(n)$ by
Lemma~\ref{article-lem:primitive-prime-mobius-count}. This gives every prime
slot $P_J(n)$.
\item For each coordinate $i$, compute the single possible ladder candidate
$\mathcal L_i^*(n)$ from
Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-arithmetic-tests}, then retain it in
$L_{i,J}(n)$ exactly for those $J$ for which its rank divides $n$ and has
full-coordinate set $J$.
\item For each coordinate permutation of each of the eight rows in
Table~\ref{tab:three-coordinate-classification}, replace the displayed slots
by the finite sets found in (i) and (ii), with
$S_i(n)=P_{\{i\}}(n)\cup L_{i,\{i\}}(n)$.
\item Keep precisely those row fillings whose selected prime powers have
distinct underlying primes, equivalently those satisfying the Hall
inequalities in Lemma~\ref{lem:finite-slot-selection}; multiply the selected
prime powers and insert the product into a set, thereby removing repetitions
caused by stabilizers and coordinate permutations.
\end{enumerate}
The products retained in (iv) are exactly the minimal generators listed in
Theorem~\ref{thm:low-support-classification-package}.  In particular, the
table is a closed finite classification for fixed $n$, not an interface to an
additional infinite search.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Step (i) is precisely the prime-slot identity in
Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-arithmetic-tests}; the M\"obius formula
there is Lemma~\ref{article-lem:primitive-prime-mobius-count}.  Step (ii) is the
ladder-slot identity in the same proposition, which says that every
$L_{i,J}(n)$ is empty or consists of the displayed single candidate.  Step
(iii) is only the coordinate-labelling convention of
Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-nonemptiness}.  By that theorem, after the
slots of a labelled row are replaced by their finite admissible sets, a row
realizes a minimal generator if and only if the slot-selection test in step
(iv) succeeds; Lemma~\ref{lem:finite-slot-selection} is exactly the stated
Hall criterion for distinct underlying primes.  The one-factor rows
$\Gamma_1$ and $\Gamma_3$ are the atomic generators with full
three-coordinate support.  Conversely, every minimal generator with
three-coordinate support has, by
Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-realization}, one fixed labelled
coordinate permutation of one of the eight row types and therefore appears in
this procedure. The labelled atomic factor set is unique, whereas the
unlabelled row is an $S_3$-orbit; the set insertion in step (iv) prevents the
raw permuted enumeration from asserting stronger uniqueness.
\end{proof}

\begin{example}[Applying the checklist at $n=42$]
\label{ex:n42-three-coordinate-checklist}
Let $n=42=2\cdot3\cdot7$ and label the coordinates by
$(\ell_1,\ell_2,\ell_3)=(2,3,7)$.  The prime factors of
\[
F_{42}=2^3\cdot13\cdot29\cdot211\cdot421
\]
have ranks
\[
\alpha(2)=3,
\alpha(4)=6,
\alpha(13)=7,
\alpha(29)=14,
\alpha(421)=21,
\alpha(211)=42.
\]
Thus $\Atom(42)=\{211\}$. The exact-rank windows used below are
$P_{12}(42)=\varnothing$, $P_{13}(42)=\{29\}$,
$P_{23}(42)=\{421\}$, and $P_{123}(42)=\{211\}$.  Singleton slots are
$S_1(42)=\varnothing$, $S_2(42)=\{2\}$, and $S_3(42)=\{13\}$.  The proper
ladder slots relevant here are
$L_{1,12}(42)=\{4\}$, $L_{1,123}(42)=\varnothing$,
$L_{2,J}(42)=\varnothing$, and $L_{3,J}(42)=\varnothing$.

The table then gives the following non-atomic generators.  The row
$\Gamma_4$ with the permuted slots $P_{13}$ and $P_{23}$ gives
$29\cdot421=12209$.  The row $\Gamma_5$ gives
$4\cdot421=1684$ from $L_{1,12}$ and $P_{23}$, while the analogous
coordinate permutation with an exact-rank $P_{13}$ partner is not a row of type
$\Gamma_5$ for this ladder orientation.  The row $\Gamma_7$ gives
$2\cdot29=58$ from $S_2$ and $P_{13}$ after relabeling.  The row $\Gamma_8$
gives $13\cdot4=52$ from $S_3$ and the permuted ladder slot $L_{1,12}$.
All other rows fail because one of the required slots is empty.  Hence
\[
\mathcal M_{42}=\{52,58,211,1684,12209\}.
\]
The example illustrates the theorem's reading rule: every success or failure
is decided by a finite slot list and a coprimeness check.
\end{example}

\section{Connected-dominance criteria}
\label{sec:supp-connected-dominance}

\begin{lemma}[Refined private-coordinate upper bound]
\label{lem:refined-private-coordinate-upper-bound}
For $m\ge2$, put $s=\omega(m)$ and $r=R(m)$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:refined-private-coordinate-upper-bound}
 \#\mathcal M_m\le U_s(r),\qquad
 U_s(r):=\sum_{t=1}^s\binom st
             \bigl(r2^{s-t}+1\bigr)^t.
\end{equation}
There is an absolute constant $K$ such that, for all $s,r\ge1$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:refined-upper-versus-covers}
 U_s(r)\le Kr^sC_s.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The case $m=2$ is immediate from $\mathcal M_2=\varnothing$ and $R(2)=1$.
Suppose $m\ge3$, and let a witness cover have $t$ atoms. Choose the canonical
private coordinate of each atom as in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{article-thm:minimal-generator-growth}; let $P$ be the resulting
$t$-set and $Q$ its complement. For a fixed $x\in P$, a prime atom has full
support $\{x\}\cup U$ for some $U\subseteq Q$, giving at most
$r2^{s-t}$ choices. A ladder atom with essential coordinate $x$ must be a
power of the index prime $x$, and the full-exponent equation in
Proposition~\ref{prop:prime-power-ladders} determines at most one such atom
over all its possible full supports. Thus the atom assigned to $x$ has at most
$r2^{s-t}+1$ choices. Summing over $P$ and $t$ proves
\eqref{eq:refined-private-coordinate-upper-bound}; ignoring coverage and
coprimeness only enlarges the count.

For $r\ge1$,
\[
 U_s(r)\le r^sU_s(1).
\]
If $t\le3s/4$, then, uniformly in this range,
\[
 (2^{s-t}+1)^t
 =2^{t(s-t)}(1+2^{-(s-t)})^t
 =(1+o(1))2^{t(s-t)}.
\]
The sum over $t>3s/4$ is
$2^{-s^2/16+O(s)}$ times a central term of
$b_s=\sum_t\binom st2^{t(s-t)}$. Hence $U_s(1)\sim b_s$. The published
asymptotic \eqref{article-eq:minimal-cover-theta} gives $C_s\sim b_s$; enlarging a
constant to cover the finitely many small values of $s$ proves
\eqref{eq:refined-upper-versus-covers}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Blockwise criterion for connected dominance]
\label{thm:blockwise-connected-dominance}
Let $(n_j)$ be a sequence, put $V_j=\mathcal P(n_j)$ and
$k_j=\omega(n_j)\to\infty$, and for nonempty $C\subseteq V_j$ put
\[
 (n_j)_C:=\prod_{\ell\in C}\ell^{\nu_\ell(n_j)}.
\]
For a nontrivial partition $\Pi\in\mathsf{Part}(V_j)$ define
\[
 \Delta(\Pi):=\sum_{\substack{C,D\in\Pi\\C<D}}|C||D|
 =\frac{k_j^2-\sum_{C\in\Pi}|C|^2}{2}.
\]
Suppose
\begin{equation}\label{eq:blockwise-window-condition}
 \varepsilon_j:=
 \max_{\substack{\Pi\in\mathsf{Part}(V_j)\\|\Pi|\ge2}}
 \frac{\sum_{C\in\Pi}|C|\log R((n_j)_C)}{\Delta(\Pi)}
 \longrightarrow0\qquad\text{\rm(BW)}.
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:connected-dominance-conclusion}
 \frac{\#(\mathcal M_{n_j}\setminus\mathcal M_{n_j}^{\rm conn})}
 {\#\mathcal M_{n_j}^{\rm conn}}
 \le\exp\left[-\left(\frac{\log2}{2}-o(1)\right)k_j\right]
 =o(1).
\end{equation}
A sufficient condition for \eqref{eq:blockwise-window-condition} is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:blockwise-window-sufficient-condition}
 \max_{\varnothing\ne C\subsetneq V_j}
 \frac{\log R((n_j)_C)}{k_j-|C|}\longrightarrow0
 \qquad\text{\rm(BW$'$)}.
\end{equation}
In particular, \eqref{eq:connected-dominance-conclusion} holds if
$R((n_j)_C)=1$ for every nonempty proper block $C$ and all sufficiently
large $j$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The unique connected-block factorization in
Theorem~\ref{article-thm:mingenerator-connected-factorization} gives
\[
 \#(\mathcal M_{n_j}\setminus\mathcal M_{n_j}^{\rm conn})
 =\sum_{\substack{\Pi\in\mathsf{Part}(V_j)\\|\Pi|\ge2}}
   \prod_{C\in\Pi}M^{\rm conn}((n_j)_C).
\]
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:refined-private-coordinate-upper-bound} to each block.
The theta asymptotic \eqref{article-eq:minimal-cover-theta} gives uniform two-sided
bounds
\[
 C_s\asymp(s+1)^{-1/2}2^{s^2/4+s}.
\]
Consequently there is an absolute constant $K_1$ such that every partition
$\Pi$ with block sizes $s_C=|C|$ contributes, after division by $C_{k_j}$,
at most
\begin{equation}\label{eq:partition-contribution-bound}
 K_1^{|\Pi|}\sqrt{k_j}\,
 \exp\left\{-\frac{\log2}{2}\Delta(\Pi)
 +\sum_{C\in\Pi}|C|\log R((n_j)_C)\right\}.
\end{equation}
Under \textup{(BW)}, this is
\[
 K_1^{|\Pi|}\sqrt{k_j}\,
 \exp\left\{-\left(\frac{\log2}{2}-o(1)\right)
 \Delta(\Pi)\right\},
\]
where the $o(1)$ is uniform over all nontrivial partitions.

If every block has size at most $k_j/2$, then
$\Delta(\Pi)\ge k_j^2/4$. There are at most $k_j^{k_j}$ set partitions,
so the total of these terms is $\exp(-\Omega(k_j^2))$. Otherwise there is a
unique largest block of size $k_j-q>k_j/2$. Then
$\Delta(\Pi)\ge q(k_j-q)$, and the remaining $q$ vertices can be selected and
partitioned in at most
\[
 \binom{k_j}{q}\mathsf B_q\le(ek_j)^q
\]
ways. Summing \eqref{eq:partition-contribution-bound} over
$1\le q<k_j/2$ therefore gives
\[
 \exp\left[-\left(\frac{\log2}{2}-o(1)\right)k_j\right]C_{k_j}.
\]
Corollary~\ref{cor:theta-refined-connected-entropy} gives
$M^{\rm conn}(n_j)\ge(1-o(1))C_{k_j}$, proving
\eqref{eq:connected-dominance-conclusion}.

Finally, if the maximum in \textup{(BW$'$)} is $\eta_j$, then every block of
a nontrivial partition is proper and
\[
 \sum_{C\in\Pi}|C|\log R((n_j)_C)
 \le\eta_j\sum_{C\in\Pi}|C|(k_j-|C|)
 =2\eta_j\Delta(\Pi).
\]
Thus \textup{(BW$'$)} implies \textup{(BW)}, and the last assertion is the
case $\eta_j=0$.
\end{proof}

Assume now that $\omega(n)=3$. Lemma~\ref{lem:fibonacci-three-coordinate-types}
reduces every witness cover to the eight listed abstract types and excludes

an abstract slot $(I,J)$ into exactly one of the displayed prime, ladder,

ladder slots $I=\{i\}\subsetneq J$ are $L_{i,J}(n)$, and singleton slots are
$S_i(n)=P_{\{i\}}(n)\cup L_{i,\{i\}}(n)$. Substituting these translations into
the eight admissible abstract types of Lemma~\ref{lem:abstract-three-types}
gives precisely the rows of Table~\ref{tab:three-coordinate-classification}.
The same substitution, with braces suppressed in prime-slot subscripts,
gives the displayed canonical-family list: the first two rows are the full
prime and full ladder families, the next three are the two-slot overlapping
families, the next two attach a singleton slot to a nonsingleton slot, and the
last row is the three-singleton family.

It remains only to identify the tests in the last column. For prime slots,
Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-arithmetic-tests} gives
$P_J(n)=\bigcup_{d\in\mathcal R_J^\circ(n)}\Pi_\alpha(d)$ and, after deleting
previously used underlying primes, the stated finite multiplicity criterion.
For ladder slots, the same proposition gives the displayed singleton-candidate
formula for $L_{i,J}(n)$. Pairwise coprimeness of the chosen prime powers is
exactly the distinct-underlying-prime condition because all slots are filled by
prime powers. Hence the table's row test is the arithmetic selection test of
Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-nonemptiness}. That theorem also gives the
converse construction and uniqueness, up to reordering slots with identical
labelled data, while Theorem~\ref{article-thm:witness-classification} identifies the
resulting products with the elements of $\mathcal M_n$.

Thus the displayed support-dependent set $\mathcal G_n$ equals
$\mathcal M_n$ in all cases $\omega(n)\le3$, including the boundary convention
$\mathcal G_2=\varnothing=\mathcal M_2$.  In the three-coordinate case the
finiteness of the prime and ladder slot sets and the exact slot enumeration
are supplied by Proposition~\ref{prop:three-coordinate-table-checklist}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Support-three obstruction certificates]
\label{cor:support-three-obstruction-certificates}
Assume $\omega(n)=3$ and let a proposed support-three minimal-generator shape be
specified by an irredundant finite list of atomic slots whose full-support sets
cover the labelled coordinates $\{1,2,3\}$.  Exactly one of the following
alternatives occurs.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item The shape is the excluded skeleton $\Gamma_2$, equivalently it requires an
atom with $1<\#E_n(\theta)<\#T_n(\theta)=3$; then no Fibonacci minimal
generator has that shape.
\item After relabeling it is one of the eight rows in
Table~\ref{tab:three-coordinate-classification}.  The row contributes exactly
the products obtained from the finite candidate sets
$\mathfrak C(P_J)$, $\mathfrak C(L_{i,J})$, and $\mathfrak C(S_i)$ by choosing
one prime power in each row slot with distinct underlying primes.
\item The row is one of the eight admissible skeletons but the finite
distinct-prime Hall inequalities of Lemma~\ref{lem:finite-slot-selection} fail;
then the row contributes no element of $\mathcal M_n$.
\end{enumerate}
Thus every failure in support three has a certificate of one of two kinds: the
single structural Fibonacci obstruction $\Gamma_2$, or a finite Hall deficiency
inside an otherwise admissible slot row.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:abstract-three-types}, every irredundant three-coordinate
witness skeleton is one of $\Gamma_1,\ldots,\Gamma_9$. The prime--ladder
dichotomy, Lemma~\ref{thm:primitive-ladder-dichotomy}, excludes precisely the
case in which an atom has a proper nonsingleton essential set inside a larger
full-support set; among the nine skeletons this is exactly $\Gamma_2$, proving
(a).

For every remaining skeleton, Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-atom-families}
translates each abstract slot into one of the prime slots $P_J(n)$, ladder
slots $L_{i,J}(n)$, or singleton slots $S_i(n)$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-arithmetic-tests}
identifies these as the finite candidate sets displayed in
Theorem~\ref{thm:low-support-classification-package}.  A selected row therefore
forms a witness cover exactly when its chosen prime powers are pairwise
coprime, and for prime powers this is exactly distinctness of underlying primes.
Lemma~\ref{lem:finite-slot-selection} is precisely the Hall criterion for such a
selection.  If the criterion holds, Theorem~\ref{article-thm:witness-classification}
turns the selected witness cover into the corresponding element of
$\mathcal M_n$; if it fails, no coprime filling of that row exists.  These are
(b) and (c), and the alternatives exhaust all support-three possibilities.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Low-support birth layers and counting]
\label{cor:low-support-normal-form}
\label{cor:low-support-birth-layer-package}
\label{prop:low-support-cardinality-formula}
Let $n\ge2$ with $\omega(n)\le3$, and let $\mathcal G_n$ be the finite
minimal-generator set described in
Theorem~\ref{thm:low-support-classification-package}.  Then $\mathcal G_n$ is
the canonical antichain of minimal elements of $B_n$, and for every $q\in D_n$,
\[
q\in B_n\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\text{there exists }g\in\mathcal G_n\text{ with }g\mid q.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
B_n=\bigcup_{g\in\mathcal G_n}\{q\in D_n:\ g\mid q\},
\]
where the union is taken inside the divisor lattice $D_n$ and is not asserted
to be disjoint.  The birth-layer cardinality is the closed finite formula
\begin{equation}\label{eq:low-support-cardinality-formula}
A(n)=
\sum_{\varnothing\ne\mathcal S\subseteq\mathcal G_n}
(-1)^{|\mathcal S|+1}
\tau\left(\frac{F_n}{\lcmop(g:g\in\mathcal S)}\right),
\end{equation}
with the convention that the empty sum is $0$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:low-support-classification-package} gives
$\mathcal G_n=\mathcal M_n$ for $\omega(n)\le3$, including the boundary
convention $\mathcal G_2=\varnothing=\mathcal M_2$. Minimal elements of an
upper set are pairwise incomparable, so $\mathcal G_n$ is an antichain in
$D_n$.  The upper-fiber identity, Lemma~\ref{article-thm:upper-fiber}, gives for every
$n\ge2$ the principal-upper-ideal decomposition
\[
B_n=\bigcup_{m\in\mathcal M_n}\{q\in D_n:m\mid q\}.
\]
Replacing $\mathcal M_n$ by $\mathcal G_n$ gives the displayed union and the
membership criterion.  The minimal elements of this union are exactly the
generators in $\mathcal G_n$, because no two elements of $\mathcal G_n$ are
comparable.

It remains to count the union.  If $\mathcal G_n=\varnothing$, then
$B_n=\varnothing$, so $A(n)=0$ and the empty-sum convention gives
\eqref{eq:low-support-cardinality-formula}.  Otherwise, for $g\in\mathcal G_n$
put $U_g:=\{q\in D_n:g\mid q\}$.  For a nonempty subset
$\mathcal S\subseteq\mathcal G_n$, a divisor $q$ of $F_n$ lies in
$\bigcap_{g\in\mathcal S}U_g$ if and only if
$\lcmop(g:g\in\mathcal S)$ divides $q$.  Since every $g\in\mathcal G_n$ divides
$F_n$, this least common multiple also divides $F_n$, and division by it gives a
bijection
\[
\bigcap_{g\in\mathcal S}U_g
\longleftrightarrow
\operatorname{Div}\left(F_n/\lcmop(g:g\in\mathcal S)\right).
\]
Hence
\[
\#\bigcap_{g\in\mathcal S}U_g=
\tau\left(\frac{F_n}{\lcmop(g:g\in\mathcal S)}\right).
\]
Ordinary finite inclusion--exclusion applied to
$B_n=\bigcup_{g\in\mathcal G_n}U_g$ proves
\eqref{eq:low-support-cardinality-formula}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Prime-only three-coordinate skeleton]
\label{cor:primitive-three-skeleton}
Assume $\mathcal P(n)=\{\ell_1,\ell_2,\ell_3\}$ and that
$n_S:=\prod_{i\in S}\ell_i^{\nu_{\ell_i}(n)}$ satisfies
$n_S\ge 3$ and $n_S\notin\{6,12\}$ for every nonempty
$S\subseteq[3]$. Then the four prime-only three-coordinate
types $\Gamma_1$, $\Gamma_4$, $\Gamma_7$, and $\Gamma_9$ are realized in
$\mathcal M_n$. More precisely, after choosing exact-rank primes
$\pi_S$ with $\alpha(\pi_S)=n_S$, the following minimal generators realize
these types:
\[
\pi_{123},\qquad
\pi_{12}\pi_{23},\qquad
\pi_1\pi_{23},\qquad
\pi_1\pi_2\pi_3 .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The hypothesis and Theorem~\ref{article-thm:primitive-divisors} provide the indicated
exact-rank primes. An exact-rank prime for $n_S$ is a prime atom with
$E_n(\pi_S)=T_n(\pi_S)=S$. The four displayed collections of coordinate
sets are precisely the prime-only types $\Gamma_1$, $\Gamma_4$,
$\Gamma_7$, and $\Gamma_9$ in Lemma~\ref{lem:abstract-three-types}, and
the chosen primes are pairwise distinct. The
pairwise-coprime selection criterion of
Lemma~\ref{lem:three-coordinate-nonemptiness} therefore gives the four
minimal generators.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:wss}
The only Wall--Sun--Sun input needed here is local.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:prime-power-ladders}, for $p\neq 2,5$ one has
$\alpha(p^2)=\alpha(p)$ if and only if $\nu_p(F_{\alpha(p)})\ge 2$, the
Wall--Sun--Sun condition for $p$ \cite{SunSun1992FibonacciFLT}.  Thus the
condition merely determines whether the first odd-prime ladder step is delayed;
we do not use any distributional assertion about such primes.
\end{remark}
